be the small set of parameters with
A\subseteq B
such that the
B
-logic topology of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
is its global logic topology. Denote by
\mathrm{cl}_{B}
the closure in the
B
-logic topology of
G
. We define by recursion the sequence
(J_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}}
of piecewise
\bigwedge_{B}
-definable normal subgroups of
G
given by
J_{0}=G^{00}_{A}
,
J_{\gamma}=\mathrm{cl}_{B}(\bigcup_{i\in\gamma}J_{i})
for
\gamma
limit and
J_{\alpha+1}=\mathrm{cl}_{B}(\bigcup\mathcal{K}_{\alpha})
where
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
is the family of piecewise
\bigwedge
-definable normal subgroups which are
\bigwedge
-definable modulo
J_{\alpha}
.

Claim
.

For
\alpha_{0}\in\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}
large enough,
J_{\alpha}=G^{\mathrm{ap}}
for any
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
.

Proof.

We prove inductively that
J_{\alpha}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
for any
\alpha\in\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}
. Obviously,
J_{0}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
. For
\gamma
limit, assuming that
J_{i}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
for each
i\in\gamma
, as
G^{\mathrm{ap}}
is piecewise
\bigwedge_{B}
-definable, we get that
J_{\gamma}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
. Finally, assuming that
J_{\alpha}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
, we have a piecewise bounded
\bigwedge
-definable onto homomorphism
\pi:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle J_{\alpha}$}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
. As
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
is aperiodic, we have that every piecewise
\bigwedge
-definable normal subgroup of
G
which is
\bigwedge
-definable modulo
J_{\alpha}
must be contained in
G^{\mathrm{ap}}
. Therefore,
\bigcup\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
, concluding that
J_{\alpha+1}\subseteq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
.

On the other hand, as there is a small amount of piecewise
\bigwedge_{B}
-definable normal subgroups of
G
, for large
\alpha_{0}
, we must have
J_{\alpha_{0}+1}=J_{\alpha_{0}}
. In particular, that means that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle J_{\alpha_{0}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle J_{\alpha_{0}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle J_{\alpha_{0}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle J_{\alpha_{0}}$}}
is an aperiodic locally compact topological group with its global logic topology, so
G^{\mathrm{ap}}\leq J_{\alpha_{0}}
. Thus,
G^{\mathrm{ap}}=J_{\beta}
for
\beta\geq\alpha_{0}
. ∎

Now, we prove by induction that, for any
\alpha\in\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}
, we have
J_{\alpha}\cap H\leq K
. Obviously,
J_{0}\cap H\leq K
. For
\gamma
limit, assuming that
J_{i}\cap H\leq K
for any
i\in\gamma
, we have
H\cap\bigcup_{i\in\gamma}J_{i}\leq K
. Therefore,
H\cap J_{\gamma}=\mathrm{cl}_{B}(H\cap\bigcup_{i\in\gamma}J_{i})\subseteq K
, as
H
is clopen and
K
is closed in the
B
-logic topology. Finally, assuming
J_{\alpha}\cap H\leq K
, we have a piecewise bounded
\bigwedge
-definable onto homomorphism
\pi:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle J_{\alpha}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle J_{\alpha}$}}\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
. As
L
is aperiodic, we have that
H\cap\bigcup\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\subseteq K
. Therefore,
H\cap J_{\alpha+1}=\mathrm{cl}_{B}(H\cap\bigcup\mathcal{K}_{\alpha})\subseteq K
, as
H
is clopen and
K
is closed in the
B
-logic topology. In particular, by the claim, we conclude that
G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap H\leq K
.
∎

Theorem 2.40
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group with a generic piece. Let
L
be the minimal
A
-Lie core of
G
and
\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}}:\ G^{0}_{A}\rightarrow L
the restriction to
G^{0}_{A}
of the global minimal Lie core map of
L
. Then,
G^{0}_{A}\cap G^{\mathrm{ap}}=\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})
. In particular,
L\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
.

Proof.

By
2.39
, we know that
G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}\leq\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})
. On the other hand,
\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})=G^{0}_{A}\cap\pi_{L}^{-1}(1)\trianglelefteq G
is
\bigwedge
-definable modulo
G^{00}_{A}\leq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
. Therefore,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
is a compact normal subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
. As it is aperiodic, we conclude that
\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})\leq G^{\mathrm{ap}}
, so
\ker(\pi_{L\mid G^{0}_{A}})=G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}
.
∎

In sum, for any piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group
G
with a generic piece, we have the following structure in terms of the components
G^{0}_{A}
,
G^{00}_{A}
and
G^{\mathrm{ap}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}1.&\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}\cap G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}&\mathrm{is\ a\ compact\ topological\ group.}\\
2.&\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}&\mathrm{is\ an\ aperiodic\ connected\ Lie\ group.}\\
3.&\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{0}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{0}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{0}_{A}$}}&\mathrm{is\ a\ totally\ disconnected\ locally\ compact}\\
&&\mathrm{topological\ group,\ i.e.\ a}\ locally\ profinite\ group.\end{array}

Unfortunately, if
G^{00}_{A}=G^{\mathrm{ap}}=G^{0}_{A}=G
, all the previous results say nothing about
G
. In the following section, we extend the Stabilizer Theorem to the context of piecewise hyperdefinable groups. This theorem gives sufficient conditions to conclude that, with enough parameters,
G^{00}
is
\bigwedge
-definable. As we pointed out at the beginning of the section, in this particular situation, the minimal Lie core gives very precise information about
G
since the quotient homomorphism is piecewise bounded and proper.

We now note that the minimal Lie core is independent of the parameters. Furthermore, as
G^{\mathrm{ap}}
is independent of expansions of the language, so is the minimal Lie core.

Corollary 2.41
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
0
-hyperdefinable group with a generic piece. Then, the minimal
A
-Lie core of
G
is isomorphic to the minimal
0
-Lie core of
G
. Furthermore, the minimal Lie core of
G
does not change by expansions of the language.

Proof.

By
2.39
, we have that the minimal
A
-Lie core of
G
is isomorphic to the minimal Lie core of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{\mathrm{ap}}$}}
. As the latter does not depend on parameters or expansions of the language, we conclude. ∎

The
A
-Lie rank

\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G)
of a piecewise hyperdefinable group
G
with a generic piece modulo
G^{00}_{A}
is the dimension of its minimal
A
-Lie core. As a consequence of
2.36
,
\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G)
is a well-defined invariant. Note that, by
2.41
, we have that
\mathrm{Lrank}(G)\coloneqq\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G)
does not depend on the parameters when
G
has a generic piece.

Proposition 2.42
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group with a generic piece modulo
G^{00}_{A}
and
N\trianglelefteq G
a piecewise
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable normal subgroup of small index. Then,

\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G)\geq\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G/N)+\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(N).

More precisely, let
L
and
L_{G/N}
be the minimal
A
-Lie cores of
G
and
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
respectively, presented with the canonical piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable structures given by
2.38
. Let
\pi_{L}
and
\pi_{L_{G/N}}
be their canonical global minimal Lie core maps. Let
(K,H)
be a minimal
A
-Yamabe pair of
G
. Then:

(1)

(K\cap N,H\cap N)
is an
A
-Yamabe pair of
N
with Lie core
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K\cap N$}}\cong L_{N}\coloneqq\pi_{L\mid H}[N]
. The connected component of
L_{N}
is aperiodic so, in particular,
\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(N)=\mathrm{dim}(L_{N})
.

(2)

There is an
\bigwedge
-definable normal subgroup
T\trianglelefteq\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
with
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}\trianglelefteq T
such that
(T,\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}})
is a minimal
A
-Yamabe pair of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
.

(3)

There is a piecewise bounded
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable surjective group homomorphism
\psi:\ L\rightarrow L_{G/N}
such that
\psi\circ\pi_{L}=\pi_{L_{G/N}}\circ\pi_{G/N}
(on the domain of
\pi_{L}
).

Proof.

Firstly, note that
G^{00}_{A}\leq N
, so we get
N^{00}_{A}=G^{00}_{A}
. We have that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K\cap N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K\cap N$}}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}\cong L_{N}
. Note that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
is a closed normal subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
. As
\widetilde{\pi}_{H/K}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
is closed between the global logic topologies, we get that
L_{N}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap N$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle((H\cap N)/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle((H\cap N)/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle((H\cap N)/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle((H\cap N)/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}
is closed in
L\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(H/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle(H/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(H/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(H/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(K/G^{00}_{A})$}}
. Therefore,
L_{N}
is a closed normal subgroup of
L
, concluding that
L_{N}
is a Lie group. Then,
(K\cap N,H\cap N)
is an
A
-Yamabe pair of
N
with Lie core (isomorphic to)
L_{N}
. Let
L_{N}^{0}
be the connected component of
L_{N}
. By
2.5
,
L^{0}_{N}
has a unique maximal compact normal subgroup
K_{N}
. Thus, by uniqueness,
K_{N}
is characteristic in
L^{0}_{N}
, which is characteristic in
L_{N}
. Therefore,
K_{N}
is a compact normal subgroup of
L
, so it is trivial by aperiodicity of
L
. In other words,
L^{0}_{N}
is aperiodic. Thus,
L^{0}_{N}
is the minimal
A
-Lie core of
N
. In particular,
\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(N)=\mathrm{dim}(L_{N})
.

(2)

By point (1), we know that
L_{N}\trianglelefteq L
is a closed subgroup, so
L_{0}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{N}$}}
is a Lie group too. As
L
is connected,
L_{0}
is connected too. By
2.5
, there is a maximal compact normal subgroup
T_{0}\trianglelefteq L_{0}
. Then,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}
is a connected aperiodic Lie group.

Since
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
is a topological group, we know that the quotient homomorphism

\pi_{(G/G^{00}_{A})/(N/G^{00}_{A})}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(G/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(N/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle(G/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle(N/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(G/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(N/G^{00}_{A})$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(G/G^{00}_{A})$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(N/G^{00}_{A})$}}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}

is an open map. Therefore,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
is an open subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
. Now,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
is small and contains a generic set, so
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
is a locally compact topological group by the Generic Set Lemma (
2.22
) and
2.20
. Thus,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
is clopen. Consider,
\phi_{0}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}\rightarrow L_{0}
given by
\phi_{0}\circ\pi_{(G/N)\mid H}=\pi_{L/L_{N}}\circ\pi_{L\mid H}
. It is clear that
\phi_{0}
is a piecewise bounded
\bigwedge
-definable surjective group homomorphism with kernel
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
, which is
\bigwedge
-definable. Therefore, it is also piecewise proper. By the Isomorphism
2.24
, it follows that
(\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}},\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}})
is an
A
-Yamabe pair of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
with Lie core (isomorphic to)
L_{0}
.

As
L_{0}
is locally hyperdefinable,
T_{0}
is
\bigwedge
-definable. Thus,
\pi_{L_{0}/T_{0}}:\ L_{0}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}
is piecewise bounded and proper
\bigwedge
-definable surjective group homomorphism. Take
\phi=\pi_{L_{0}/T_{0}}\circ\phi_{0}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}
. Then,
\phi
is a piecewise bounded and proper
\bigwedge
-definable surjective group homomorphism. By the Isomorphism
2.24
, we conclude that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}\cong\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(H/N)$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle(H/N)$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(H/N)$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(H/N)$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T$}}
where
T\coloneqq\ker(\phi)
is an
\bigwedge
-definable normal subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
with
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}\trianglelefteq T
. Consequently,
(T,\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}})
is a minimal
A
-Yamabe pair of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}}
with Lie core (isomorphic to)
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}\cong L_{G/N}
.

(3)

By the Isomorphism
2.24
, take
\eta:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle T_{0}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L_{0}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T_{0}$}}\rightarrow L_{G/N}
isomorphism such that
\pi_{L_{G/N}\mid(\mathchoice{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}})}=\eta\circ\phi
. Consider
\psi=\eta\circ\pi_{L_{0}/T_{0}}\circ\pi_{L/L_{N}}:\ L\rightarrow L_{G/N}
— note that, a priori, the definition of
\psi
depends on
(K,H)
. Obviously,
\psi
is a piecewise bounded
\bigwedge
-definable onto group homomorphism. Also,
\psi\circ\pi_{L\mid H}=\pi_{L_{G/N}\mid(\mathchoice{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\displaystyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\textstyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle N$}}{\raise 1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-0.81949pt/\kern-0.81949pt\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle N$}})}\circ\pi_{(G/N)\mid H}
.

Let
(K^{\prime},H^{\prime})
be any other minimal
A
-Yamabe pair of
G
. For
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
, there is
h\in H\cap H^{\prime}
such that
\pi_{L}(h^{\prime})=\pi_{L}(h)
, i.e.
h^{-1}h^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}
. By point (2), it follows that
\pi_{L_{G/N}}(\pi_{G/N}(h))=\pi_{L_{G/N}}(\pi_{G/N}(h^{\prime}))
. Thus,
\psi(\pi_{L}(h^{\prime}))=\psi(\pi_{L}(h))=\pi_{L_{G/N}}(\pi_{G/N}(h))=\pi_{L_{G/N}}(\pi_{G/N}(h^{\prime}))
. Therefore,
\psi\circ\pi_{L}=\pi_{L_{G/N}}\circ\pi_{G/N}
on the domain of
\pi_{L}
.

It remains to show that
\psi
is
A
-invariant. Take
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{M}/A)
and
x\in L
. Take
h
such that
\pi_{L}(h)=x
. Using that
\pi_{L}
,
\pi_{L_{G/N}}
and
\pi_{G/N}
are
A
-invariant, we get that

\psi(\sigma(x))=\psi\circ\pi_{L}(\sigma(h))=\pi_{L_{G/N}}\circ\pi_{G/N}(\sigma(x))=\sigma(\pi_{L_{G/N}}\circ\pi_{G/N}(x))=\sigma(\psi(h)).

Finally, putting everything together, we conclude that

\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G/N)=\mathrm{dim}(L_{G/N})\leq\mathrm{dim}(L_{0})=\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(G)-\mathrm{Lrank}_{A}(N).

∎

The following proposition is a direct consequence of the results of
[
JTZ23
]
. I am very grateful to Chieu-Minh for telling me about it.

Proposition 2.43

(An-Jing-Tran-Zhang bound)
.

Let
G
be a piecewise hyperdefinable group and
X
a symmetric generic set of
G
. Then,
\mathrm{Lrank}(G)\leq 12\log_{2}(k)^{2}
where
k=[X^{2}:X]
.

Proof.

By working modulo
G^{00}
, we may assume that
G
is small. Let
(K,H)
be a minimal Yamabe pair of
G
and
\pi\coloneqq\pi_{H/K}:H\rightarrow L
the minimal Lie core. By the Generic Set Lemma (
2.22
), we have that
X^{2}
is a symmetric compact neighbourhood of the identity in the global logic topology. Thus, as
H
is open,
Y=H\cap X^{2}
is also a symmetric compact neighbourhood of the identity in the global logic topology. By
[
Mac23
, Lemma 2.3]
,
Y
is a
k^{3}
-approximate subgroup. As
\pi
is a continuous and open homomorphism,
\pi[Y]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity and a
k^{3}
-approximate subgroup. As it is a neighbourhood of the identity, it has positive Haar measure, so the general Brunn-Minkowski Inequality
[
JTZ23
, Theorem 1.1]
applies and we get that
2\leq k^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{\alpha}}}
with
\alpha\coloneqq d-m-h
, where
d=\dim(L)
,
m=\max\{\dim(\Gamma)\mathrel{:}\Gamma\leq L\mathrm{\ compact}\}
and
h
is the helix dimension of
L
. On the other hand, by
[
JTZ23
, Corollary 2.15]
,
h\leq\nicefrac{{n}}{{3}}
with
n\coloneqq d-m
, so
2n\leq 9\log_{2}(k)
. Finally, as
L
has no compact normal subgroups, by
[
AJTZ21
, Fact 3.6, Lemma 3.9]
, we conclude that
d\leq\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
, so

d\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\lfloor\frac{9}{2}\log_{2}(k)\right\rfloor^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\lfloor\frac{9}{2}\log_{2}(k)\right\rfloor\leq 12\log_{2}(k)^{2}.

∎

2.4.1.
Digression on Breuillard-Green-Tao Classification

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a particular class of piecewise hyperdefinable groups and
L
a finite dimensional Lie group. It is then natural to study the
direct problem
for
\mathcal{A}
or (dually) the
inverse problem
for
L
:

What are the Lie cores of elements of
\mathcal{A}
?

Is
L
a Lie core of an element of
\mathcal{A}
?

The classification theorem by Breuillard, Green and Tao for finite approximate subgroups
[
BGT12
]
can be interpreted as an answer to the direct problem for the class of piecewise definable subgroups generated by pseudo-finite definable approximate subgroups. Indeed, it can be restated to say that Lie cores of piecewise definable subgroups generated by pseudo-finite definable approximate subgroups are nilpotent
[
BGT12
, Proposition 9.6]
. We consider it interesting to discuss the direct and inverse problems in general. In fact, one may expect that solutions of these questions would yield classification results similar to the one of
[
BGT12
]
.

The following easy examples show that the inverse problem is trivially solved for some basic classes of piecewise hyperdefinable groups. In particular, these examples show that no general classification result for the Lie cores analogous to the one of
[
BGT12
]
could be found in those cases.

Example 2.44
.

Any Lie group
L
is a Lie core of some piecewise hyperdefinable group in the theory of real closed fields. Indeed,
L
is in particular a second countable manifold, so it is a locally hyperdefinable subset of countable cofinality. By
1.35
, the group operations are piecewise bounded
\bigwedge
-definable, so
L
is a locally hyperdefinable group. Clearly,
L
is its own Lie core.

For a slightly more explicit construction, note that any connected Lie group
L
is the Lie core of some piecewise definable group generated by a definable approximate subgroup. Indeed, connected Lie groups are metrisable by Birkhoff-Kakutani Theorem
[
Tao14
, Theorem 1.5.2]
, so take a left invariant metric
d
for
L
. As
L
is locally compact, we may assume that the closed unit ball
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is a compact symmetric neighbourhood of the identity. Consider the structure of
L
with the language of groups, a sort for
\mathbb{R}
with the language of ordered rings and a function symbol for the metric
d
. Let
L^{\prime}
be an
|L|
-saturated elementary extension of it. Consider the subgroup
H\leq L^{\prime}
generated by the closed unit ball and
E=\{(a,b)\mathrel{:}d(a,b)<\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}\mathrm{\ for\ any\ }n\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Clearly,
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
is a definable approximate subgroup and
H
is the piecewise definable group generated by it. Then,
L
is a Lie core of
H
, as, in fact, we have
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\cong L
.

In the case of a linear connected Lie group
L
, we can combine both examples. Indeed, in that case, in the real numbers,
L
is piecewise definable and its metric and group operations are definable, so, after saturation, we just need to take the piecewise definable group generated by the closed unit ball and quotient out by the infinitesimals as in the previous example.

3.
Stabilizer Theorem

In this section, we aim to extend the Stabilizer Theorem
[
Hru12
, Theorem 3.5]
to piecewise hyperdefinable groups. The main point of this theorem is that it provides sufficient conditions to conclude that
G^{00}
is
\bigwedge
-definable. As we already noted in the previous section, this result has significant consequences for the projection
\pi_{L}
of the minimal Lie core.

To prove the Stabilizer Theorem, we need first to extend the model-theoretic notions of dividing, forking and stable relation to piecewise hyperdefinable sets. Once these model-theoretic notions have been properly defined for piecewise hyperdefinable groups, adapting the original proof of the Stabilizer Theorem is straightforward.

Forking and dividing for hyperimaginaries have already been well studied by many authors (e.g.
[
HKP00
,
Wag10
,
Kim14
]
). Here, in the first subsection, we rewrite the definitions of dividing and forking for hyperdefinable sets in a slightly different way which we find more natural from the point of view of this paper. After that, it is trivial to extend the definition to the context of piecewise hyperdefinable sets.

3.1.
Dividing and forking

Let
(P_{t})_{t\in T}
be
A
-hyperdefinable sets. Write
P\coloneqq\prod P_{t}
. For infinite tuples
\overline{a},\overline{b}\in P
of hyperimaginaries, we write
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{a}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/A)
to mean that
\mathrm{tp}(a_{\mid T_{0}}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(b_{\mid T_{0}}/A)
for any
T_{0}\subseteq T
finite.

Let
(I,<)
be a linear order. An
A
-indiscernible
sequence in
P
indexed by
(I,<)
is a sequence
(\overline{a}_{i})_{i\in I}
of hyperimaginary tuples
\overline{a}_{i}\in P
such that, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
i_{1}<\cdots<i_{n}
and
j_{1}<\cdots<j_{n}
,

\mathrm{tp}(\overline{a}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\overline{a}_{i_{n}}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(\overline{a}_{j_{1}},\ldots,\overline{a}_{j_{n}}/A).

Lemma 3.1

(Standard Lemma)
.

Let
P=\prod_{t\in T}P_{t}
be a product of
A
-hyperdefinable sets and
(I,<)
and
(J,<)
two infinite linear orders, with
|T|^{+}+|J|\leq\kappa
. Then, given a sequence
(\overline{a}_{i})_{i\in I}
of hyperimaginary tuples in
P
, for any set of representatives
A^{*}
, there is a sequence
(\overline{b}_{j})_{j\in J}
of hyperimaginary tuples in
P
with an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence of representatives
(\overline{b}^{*}_{j})_{j\in J}
such that

({\overline{b}_{j_{1}}}_{\mid T_{0}},\ldots,{\overline{b}_{j_{n}}}_{\mid T_{0}})\in W

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, any
j_{1}<\ldots<j_{n}
, any
T_{0}\subseteq T
finite and any
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable set
W\subseteq\left(\prod_{t\in T_{0}}P_{t}\right)^{n}
such that
({\overline{a}_{i_{1}}}_{\mid T_{0}},\ldots,{\overline{a}_{i_{n}}}_{\mid T_{0}})\in W
for every
i_{1}<\cdots<i_{n}
.

Proof.

Trivial from the classic Ehrenfeucht-Mostowski Standard Lemma proved using Ramsey’s Theorem
[
TZ12
, Theorem 5.1.5]
.
∎

Corollary 3.2
.

Let
P=\prod_{t\in T}P_{t}
be a product of
A
-hyperdefinable sets and
(I,<)
an infinite linear order, with
|T|^{+}+|I|\leq\kappa
. Then, a sequence
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in I}
of hyperimaginary tuples in
P
is
A
-indiscernible if and only if, for some set of representatives
A^{*}
of
A
, there is an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence of representatives
(\overline{b}^{*}_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Furthermore, if
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in I}
is
A
-indiscernible, for any set of representatives
A^{*}
there is an
A^{**}
-indiscernible sequence of representatives
(\overline{b}^{*}_{i})_{i\in I}
of
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in I}
where
A^{**}
is another set of representatives of
A
with
\mathrm{tp}(A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(A^{**})
.

Proof.

By the Standard
3.1
and
1.10
.
∎

Let
P=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}
be an
A
-hyperdefinable set and
\mathrm{quot}_{P}:\ X\rightarrow P
its quotient map. An
\bigwedge
-definable subset
V\subseteq P

divides over
A
if and only if
\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P}[V]
divides over
A^{*}
for some set of representatives of
A
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
P
be an
A
-hyperdefinable set and
V\subseteq P
an
\bigwedge_{A,B}
-definable subset. Then,
V
divides over
A
if and only if there are a finite tuple
b
from
B
and an
\bigwedge_{A,b}
-definable subset
W\subseteq P
such that
V\subseteq W
and
W
divides over
A
.

Proof.

One direction is trivial. Let us check the other. Take a uniform definition
\underline{V}
of
V
, which exists by
1.11
. Take representatives
A^{*}
of
A
such that
\underline{V}(x,A^{*},B^{*})
divides over
A^{*}
. There is then
b
finite such that
\underline{W}(x,A^{*},b^{*})\coloneqq\underline{V}(x,A^{*},B^{*})\cap\mathrm{For}^{x}(\mathtt{L}(A^{*},b^{*}))
divides over
A^{*}
. Now, as
\underline{V}
is a uniform definition,
W
is
\bigwedge_{A,b}
-definable. Obviously,
V\subseteq W
and
W
divides over
A
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
P
and
Q
be
A
-hyperdefinable sets. Let
V\subseteq Q\times P
be an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable set and
b\in Q
. Then,
V(b)
divides over
A
if and only if there is an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
of hyperimaginaries from
Q
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b_{0}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(b/A)
and
\bigcap_{i\in\omega}V(b_{i})=\emptyset
.

Furthermore,
V(b)
divides over
A
if and only if for any set of representatives
A^{*}
of
A
there is another set of representatives
A^{**}
such that
\mathrm{tp}(A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(A^{**})
and
V(b)
divides over
A^{**}
.

Proof.

Assume
V(b)
divides over
A
. Take a uniform definition
\underline{V}
of
V
, which exists by
1.11
. Take representatives
A^{*}
of
A
such that
\underline{V}(x,A^{*},b^{*})
divides over
A^{*}
. There is then an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence
(b^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b^{*}_{0}/A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(b^{*}/A^{*})
and
\bigcup_{i\in\omega}\underline{V}(x,A^{*},b^{*}_{i})
is not finitely satisfiable. Then,
(b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
given by
b_{i}=\mathrm{quot}_{Q}(b^{*}_{i})
is
A
-indiscernible and
\mathrm{tp}(b_{0}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(b/A)
. Also,
\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P}\left[\bigcap_{i\in\omega}V(b_{i})\right]=\bigcap_{i\in\omega}\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P}[V(b_{i})]=\bigcap_{i\in\omega}\underline{V}(\mathfrak{M},A^{*},b^{*}_{i})=\emptyset
, so
\bigcap_{i\in\omega}V(b_{i})=\emptyset
by surjectivity of
\mathrm{quot}_{P}
.

On the other hand, assume there is an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
in
Q
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b_{0}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(b/A)
and
\bigcap_{i\in\omega}V(b_{i})=\emptyset
. Take a uniform definition
\underline{V}
of
V
, which exists by
1.11
. By
3.2
, there is an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence
(b^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
of representatives of
(b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
with
A^{*}
representatives of
A
. Now, as
\mathrm{tp}(b_{0}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(b/A)
, by
1.10
, there is a representative
b^{**}\in b
and a set of representatives
A^{**}
of
A
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b^{*}_{0},A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(b^{**},A^{**})
. Take
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{M})
mapping
(b_{0}^{*},A^{*})
to
(b^{**},A^{**})
, and write
b^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq\sigma(b_{i})
and
{b^{\prime}}^{*}_{i}\coloneqq\sigma(b^{*}_{i})
for
i\in\omega
. Then,
({b^{\prime}}^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
is an
A^{**}
-indiscernible sequence with
{b^{\prime}}^{*}_{0}=b^{**}
. For this sequence, it follows that

\emptyset=\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P}\left[\bigcap_{i\in\omega}V(b^{\prime}_{i})\right]=\bigcap_{i\in\omega}\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P}[V(b^{\prime}_{i})]=\bigcap_{i\in\omega}\underline{V}(\mathfrak{M},A^{**},{b^{\prime}}^{*}_{i}),

concluding that
\underline{V}
divides over
A^{**}
. In particular,
V
divides over
A
.

For the “furthermore part”, note that in the previous paragraph
\mathrm{tp}(A^{*})
can be chosen arbitrarily by
3.2
.
∎

Combining both propositions we conclude that the definition given here is equivalent to the one studied previously by other authors (e.g.
[
HKP00
,
Wag10
,
Kim14
]
). It is now straightforward to prove the following basic proposition.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
P
and
Q
be
A
-hyperdefinable sets. Let
V\subseteq P
be
\bigwedge
-definable and
f:\ P\rightarrow Q
a
1
-to-
1

\bigwedge_{A}
-definable function. Then,
f[V]
divides over
A
provided that
V
divides over
A
.

Let
P
be an
A
-hyperdefinable set. The
forking ideal

\mathfrak{f}_{P}(A)
of
P
over
A
is the ideal of
\bigwedge
-definable subsets of
P
generated by the ones dividing over
A
. An
\bigwedge
-definable subset of
P

forks over
A
if it is in that forking ideal.

Remark 3.6
.

Trivially, we have that
V
forks over
A
implies that
\underline{V}
forks over
A
. In the case of simple theories, the converse also holds as forking and dividing are the same
[
Wag10
, Proposition 3.2.7]
. In general, however, the converse is not true. For instance, let
\mathfrak{M}
be the monster model of the theory of dense circular orders in the usual language, let
M
be the whole
1
-ary universe of
\mathfrak{M}
and
E
the trivial equivalence relation given by
xEy
for every
x,y\in M
. Obviously,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle M$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}
is a singleton, so it does not fork over
\emptyset
. However,
M
forks over
\emptyset
.

Let
P
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable set. An
\bigwedge
-definable set
V\subseteq P

divides
over
A
if it divides over
A
as subset of some/any piece
P_{i}
containing
V
. Note that, by
3.5
, this is well-defined. An
\bigwedge
-definable subset
V
of
P
forks over
A
if and only if
V
forks over
A
as subset of some/any piece
P_{i}
containing
V
. The
forking ideal

\mathfrak{f}_{P}(A)
of
P
over
A
is the family of
\bigwedge
-definable subsets of
P
forking over
A
. Clearly,
\mathfrak{f}_{P}(A)
is the ideal of
\bigwedge
-definable subsets of
P
generated by the ones dividing over
A
.

3.2.
Ideals

From now on,
\lambda
is a cardinal with
\kappa\geq\lambda>|\mathtt{L}|+|A|
.

Let
P
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable set and
\mu
an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets in
P
. We say that
\mu
is
A
-invariant
if it is invariant under
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{M}/A)
, i.e.
W(\overline{b})\in\mu
implies
W(\overline{b}^{\prime})\in\mu
for any
\overline{b}^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/A)=\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}^{\prime}/A)
and any
\bigwedge_{\bar{b}}
-definable subset
W(\overline{b})\subseteq P
with
|\overline{b}|<\lambda
. We say that an
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subset is
\mu
-negligible
if it is in
\mu
and that it is
\mu
-wide
if it is not in
\mu
. We say that
\mu
is
locally atomic
if, for any wide
\bigwedge_{B}
-definable subset
V
with
|B|<\lambda
, there is
a\in V
such that
\mathrm{tp}(a/B)
is wide.

Remark 3.7
.

For
X\subseteq P
, write
\mu_{\mid X}\coloneqq\{W\in\mu\mathrel{:}W\subseteq X\}
. Clearly,
\mu_{\mid X}
is an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
X
. Say
P=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,P_{i}
, then write
\mu_{i}\coloneqq\mu_{\mid P_{i}}
for each piece. We have from the definitions that
\mu
is
A
-invariant if and only if each
\mu_{i}
is so. Similarly, it is locally atomic if and only if each
\mu_{i}
is so.

We say that an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subset
V
is
A
-medium
if for any
\bigwedge_{\bar{b}}
-definable subset
W(\overline{b})\subseteq V
with
|\bar{b}|<\lambda
, we have

W(\overline{b})\in\mu\Leftrightarrow W(\overline{b}_{0})\cap W(\overline{b}_{1})\in\mu

for any
A
-indiscernible sequence
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
realising
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/A)
. We say that an
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subset
V
is
A
-medium if there is an
A
-medium
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subset
V_{0}
with
V\subseteq V_{0}
. We say that
V
is
strictly
A
-medium
if it is wide and
A
-medium. We say that
\mu
is
A
-medium
4
4

4

We use the terminology of
[
MOS20
]
. In
[
Hru12
]
, it is said that the ideal has the
S_{1}
property.
if every
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subset is
A
-medium for
\mu
.

Remark 3.8
.

Note that, by definition, if
\mu
is
A
-medium,
\mu
is in particular
A
-invariant.
Note that the family of
A
-medium sets of
\mu
is an
A
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets.

Example 3.9
.

The forking ideal over
A
is an
A
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge
-definable subsets. In simple theories, the forking ideal over
A
is locally atomic and
A
-medium. Indeed, it is locally atomic by the extension property of forking
[
Wag10
, Theorem 3.2.8]
. On the other hand, suppose
V(\overline{b})
is
\bigwedge_{\overline{b}}
-definable and
V(\overline{b}_{0})\cap V(\overline{b}_{1})
forks over
A
with
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
an
A
-indiscernible sequence realising
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/A)
. Then, by simplicity,
V(\overline{b}_{0})\cap V(\overline{b}_{1})
divides over
A
, so
\bigcap^{n}_{i=0}V(\overline{b}_{2i})\cap V(\overline{b}_{2i+1})=\emptyset
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus,
V(\overline{b})
divides over
A
, so it forks over
A
. As
V
is arbitrary, we conclude that the forking ideal over
A
is
A
-medium.

(2)

If we have an
A
-invariant measure on the lattice of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable sets, the ideal of zero measure
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets is an
A
-invariant ideal. In this case, every
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subset of finite measure is
A
-medium.

Lemma 3.10
.

[
Hru12
, Lemma 2.9]

Let
P
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable set and
\mu
an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets on
P
. Let
V\subseteq P
be a strictly
A
-medium
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subset. Then,
V
does not fork over
A
.

Proof.

Take an
A
-medium
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable set
V_{0}
such that
V\subseteq V_{0}
. Suppose that
V
forks over
A
. Then, there are
W^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,W^{\prime}_{n}

\bigwedge
-definable subsets dividing over
A
such that
V\subseteq\bigcup_{i}W^{\prime}_{i}
. Applying
3.3
, we may assume that each
W^{\prime}_{i}
is
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable. Now,
V\subseteq\bigcup(W^{\prime}_{i}\cap V_{0})
and
W^{\prime}_{i}\cap V_{0}
divides over
A
. Write
W_{i}(\overline{b})=W^{\prime}_{i}\cap V_{0}
. By
3.4
, there is
(\overline{b}_{j})_{j\in\omega}

A
-indiscernible such that
\bigcap_{j\in\omega}W_{i}(\overline{b}_{j})=\emptyset
. Hence,
\bigcap^{k}_{j=0}W_{i}(\overline{b}_{j})=\emptyset\in\mu
for some
k
, concluding that
W_{i}(\overline{b})\in\mu
by
A
-mediumness of
V_{0}
. This concludes that
V\in\mu
, contradicting that
V
is wide.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}\preceq\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
and
G=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,G_{k}
be a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group. Let
\mu
be an
N
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
. Let
W
and
U
be non-empty
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subsets of
G
. If
\mu
is invariant under left translations and
U\cdot W
is
N
-medium, then
W
is
N
-medium too. Similarly, if
\mu
is invariant under right translations and
W\cdot U
is medium over
A
, then
W
is medium over
A
too.

Proof.

Let
X(\overline{b})\subseteq W
be
\bigwedge_{\bar{b}}
-definable with
|\overline{b}|<\lambda
. Take an
N
-indiscernible sequence
(\overline{b}^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
of representatives of elements in
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/N)
and write
X_{i}\coloneqq X(\overline{b}_{i})
. Fix
k
such that
U\subseteq G_{k}
and take
\underline{U}
defining
U
. As
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
,
\underline{U}
is finitely satisfiable in
N
, so
\underline{U}
does not fork over
N

[
TZ12
, Lemma 7.1.10]
. Thus,
\underline{U}
has a non-forking extension to a complete type
p
over
N,\overline{b}^{*}_{0}
. As
p
does not fork over
N
, it does not divide over
N
. By
[
TZ12
, Lemma 7.1.5]
, there is
a^{*}
realising
p
such that
(\overline{b}^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
is
N,a^{*}
-indiscernible. As
a^{*}
realises
p
, in particular,
a\in U
.

Suppose
\mu
is invariant under left translations and
U\cdot W
is
N
-medium. Then, we get that
a\cdot X_{0}\cap a\cdot X_{1}\in\mu
if and only if
a\cdot X_{0}\in\mu
. Therefore, provided left translational invariance,

X_{0}\cap X_{1}\in\mu\Leftrightarrow a\cdot X_{0}\cap a\cdot X_{1}\in\mu\Leftrightarrow a\cdot X_{0}\in\mu\Leftrightarrow X_{0}\in\mu.

We similarly prove the case with right translations.∎

3.3.
Stable relations

Let
P
and
Q
be piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable sets and
V,W\subseteq P\times Q
disjoint
A
-invariant subsets. We say that
V
and
W
are
unstably separated over
A
if there is an infinite
A
-indiscernible sequence
(a_{i},b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
such that
(a_{0},b_{1})\in V
and
(a_{1},b_{0})\in W
. We say that they are
stably separated over
A
if they are not unstably separated. We say that an
A
-invariant binary relation
R\subseteq P\times Q
is
stable over
A
if
R
and
R^{c}
are stably separated over
A
.

Remark 3.12
.

Clearly, being stably separated over
A
is invariant under piecewise
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable isomorphisms.

(2)

Note that
V
and
W
are stably separated over
A
if and only if
V\cap(P_{i}\times Q_{j})
and
W\cap(P_{i}\times Q_{j})
are stably separated over
A
for any pieces
P_{i}
and
Q_{j}
.

(3)

Let
P
and
Q
be
A
-hyperdefinable. By
3.2
,
V
and
W
are stably separated over
A
if and only if
\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P\times Q}[V]
and
\mathrm{quot}^{-1}_{P\times Q}[W]
are stably separated over
A^{*}
for any set of representatives
A^{*}
of
A
. In particular, if
V
and
W
are
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable, we have that
V
and
W
are stably separated over
A
if and only if
\underline{V}
and
\underline{W}
are stably separated over
A^{*}
for any set of representatives
A^{*}
of
A
and any partial types
\underline{V}
and
\underline{W}
defining
V
and
W
.

(4)

Note that, by the symmetry of stability,
R
is stable over
A
if and only if
R^{c}
is stable over
A
.

We need the following two lemmas from
[
Hru12
]
for the Stabilizer Theorem. Their original proofs can be easily adapted.

Recall that we say that a partial type
\Sigma(x,y)
over
A^{*}
divides over
A^{*}

with respect to
a global
A^{*}
-invariant type
\widehat{q}(y)
if there is a sequence
(b^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b^{*}_{i}/A^{*},b^{*}_{0},\ldots,b^{*}_{i-1})\models\widehat{q}
and
\bigwedge\Sigma(x,b^{*}_{i})
is inconsistent.

Lemma 3.13
.

[
Hru12
, Lemma 2.2]
Let
P=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}
and
Q=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle Y$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle F$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle Y$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle F$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Y$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle F$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Y$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F$}}
be
A^{*}
-hyperdefinable sets and
\widehat{q}
a global
A^{*}
-invariant type with
\underline{Y}\in\widehat{q}
. Let
W_{1},W_{2}\subseteq P\times Q
be
\bigwedge_{A^{*}}
-definable sets stably separated over
A^{*}
. Assume that
a\in P
is such that
\underline{W_{2}(a)}\subseteq\widehat{q}
. Then,
V=W_{1}\cap\left(\mathrm{tp}(a/A^{*})\times Q\right)
divides over
A^{*}
. Furthermore,
\underline{V}
divides over
A^{*}
with respect to
\widehat{q}
.

For sets
V
and
W
, write

V\times_{\mathrm{nf}(A)}W\coloneqq\{(a,b)\in V\times W\mathrel{:}\mathrm{tp}(b/aA)\mbox{ does not fork over }A\}.

Similarly,
V\times_{\mathrm{ndiv}(A)}W
,
V\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(A)}{\times}$ \hss}W
and
V\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{ndiv}(A)}{\times}$ \hss}W
.

Lemma 3.14
.

[
Hru12
, Lemma 2.3]

Let
P
and
Q
be piecewise
A^{*}
-hyperdefinable sets and
R\subseteq P\times Q
a stable binary relation over
A^{*}
. Let
p\subseteq P
and
q\subseteq Q
be types over
A^{*}
. Assume that there is an
A^{*}
-invariant global type
\widehat{q}
extending a partial type
\underline{q}
over
A^{*}
defining
q
.

(1)

Take
a\in p
and
b\in q
such that
(a,b)\in R
. Suppose that there are representatives
a^{*}\in a
and
b^{*}\in b
such that
b^{*}\models\widehat{q}_{\mid A^{*},a^{*}}
. Then, we have that
(a^{\prime},b)\in R
for any
a^{\prime}\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/A^{*},b)
does not divide over
A^{*}
.

(2)

Take
a^{\prime},a\in p
and
b\in q
. Suppose that
\mathrm{tp}(a/A^{*},b)
and
\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/A^{*},b)
do not divide over
A^{*}
. Then,
(a,b)\in R
if and only if
(a^{\prime},b)\in R
.

(3)

Assume that there is also an
A^{*}
-invariant global type
\widehat{p}
extending a partial type
\underline{p}
over
A^{*}
defining
p
. Then, the following conditions are equivalent:

\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathrm{i.}&p\times_{\mathrm{ndiv}(A^{*})}q\cap R\neq\emptyset&\mathrm{v.}&p\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{ndiv}(A^{*})}{\times}$ \hss}q\cap R\neq\emptyset\\
\mathrm{ii.}&p\times_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}q\cap R\neq\emptyset&\mathrm{vi.}&p\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}{\times}$ \hss}q\cap R\neq\emptyset\\
\mathrm{iii.}&p\times_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}q\subseteq R&\mathrm{vii.}&p\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}{\times}$ \hss}q\subseteq R\\
\mathrm{iv.}&p\times_{\mathrm{ndiv}(A^{*})}q\subseteq R&\mathrm{viii.}&p\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{ndiv}(A^{*})}{\times}$ \hss}q\subseteq R\end{array}

3.4.
Stabilizer Theorem

From now on,
\lambda
is a cardinal with
\kappa\geq\lambda>|\mathtt{L}|+|A|
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
. Let
V,W\subseteq G
be two
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets. The
(left)
\mu
-stabilizer of
V
with respect to
W
is the set
\mathrm{St}_{\mu}(V,W)=\{a\in G\mathrel{:}a^{-1}\cdot V\cap W\notin\mu\}
, and the
(left)
\mu
-stabilizer group of
V
with respect to
W
is the subgroup
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mu}(V,W)
of
G
generated by
\mathrm{St}_{\mu}(V,W)
. Write
\mathrm{St}_{\mu}(V)\coloneqq\mathrm{St}_{\mu}(V,V)
and
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mu}(V)\coloneqq\mathrm{St}_{\mu}(V,V)
. We omit the subscript
\mu
if there is no confusion.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group,
\mu
an
A
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
and
V
and
W
two
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable wide subsets. Then:

(1)

Take any
B\supseteq A
with
|B|<\lambda
. If
\mu
is locally atomic,
g\in\mathrm{St}(V,W)
if and only if there are
a\in V
and
b\in W
such that
g=ab^{-1}
with
\mathrm{tp}(b/B,g)\notin\mu
.

(2)

If
\mu
is invariant under left translations, then
\mathrm{St}(V,W)^{-1}=\mathrm{St}(W,V)
. In particular,
\mathrm{St}(V)
is a symmetric subset.

We say that a subset
X\subseteq G
is
stable over
A
if the relation
\{(a,b)\mathrel{:}a^{-1}b\in X\}
is stable over
A
.

Example 3.16
.

[
Hru12
, Lemma 2.10]
&
[
MOS20
, Lemma 2.8]
Let
\mu
be an
A
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group. If
\mu
is invariant under left translations and
V
and
W
are
A
-medium
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subsets, then
\mathrm{St}(V,W)
is stable over
A
.

Remark 3.17
.

Since
{\scriptstyle\bullet}^{-1}:\ G\rightarrow G
is a piecewise
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable isomorphism,
X
is stable over
A
if and only if
X^{-1}
is stable over
A
. Also,
X
is stable over
A
if and only if
\{(a,b)\mathrel{:}ab\in X\}
is stable over
A
, if and only if
\{(a,b)\mathrel{:}ab^{-1}\in X\}
is stable over
A
.

For subsets
V
and
W
of a piecewise hyperdefinable group
G
, write

V\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(A)}W\coloneqq\{a\cdot b\mathrel{:}(a,b)\in V\times_{\mathrm{nf}(A)}W\}.

Similarly,
V\cdot_{\mathrm{ndiv}(A)}W
,
V\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(A)}{\cdot}$ \hss}W
and
V\hbox{\hss$\prescript{}{\mathrm{ndiv}(A)}{\cdot}$ \hss}W
.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A^{*}
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
a locally atomic
A^{*}
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
which is also invariant under left translations. Let
p
be an
A^{*}
-medium
A^{*}
-type and
X\subseteq G
a stable subset over
A^{*}
containing
p
. Suppose that there is an
A^{*}
-invariant global type
\widehat{p}
extending a partial type
\underline{p}
over
A^{*}
defining
p
. Then,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\subseteq X\cdot X^{-1}
. Furthermore,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}p\subseteq X
.

Proof.

We may assume that
p
is strictly
A
-medium; otherwise,
\mathrm{St}(p)=\emptyset
and
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\{1\}
, so the lemma holds trivially. Since
\mu
is invariant under left translations,
\mathrm{St}(p)^{-1}=\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.15
(
2
). Thus, by definition,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\bigcup^{\infty}_{n=0}\mathrm{St}(p)^{n}
. We prove by induction on
n
that
b\cdot c\in X
for
b\in\mathrm{St}(p)^{n}
and
c\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(c/A^{*},b)
does not fork over
A^{*}
.

By hypothesis,
p\subseteq X
, so we are done for
n=0
. Assume it is true for
n-1
with
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
. Take
b=b_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot b_{n}
with
b_{i}\in\mathrm{St}(p)
for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
c\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(c/A^{*},b)
does not fork over
A^{*}
. We want to prove that
b\cdot c\in X
.
As
b_{n}\in\mathrm{St}(p)
, by
3.15
(
1
), there is
c^{\prime}\in p
such that
b_{n}\cdot c^{\prime}\in p
and
\mathrm{tp}(c^{\prime}/A^{*},b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})\notin\mu
. In particular, as
p
is
A^{*}
-medium,
\mathrm{tp}(c^{\prime}/A^{*},b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
does not fork over
A^{*}
by
3.10
. Hence,
\mathrm{tp}(c^{\prime}/A^{*},b)
and
\mathrm{tp}(b_{n}\cdot c^{\prime}/A^{*},b_{1},\ldots,b_{n-1})
do not fork over
A^{*}
. By induction hypothesis,
b\cdot c^{\prime}=(b_{1}\cdots b_{n-1})\cdot b_{n}\cdot c^{\prime}\in X
. Since
(b,c^{\prime})\in\mathrm{tp}(b/A^{*})\times_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}p
, by
3.14
(
2
), we conclude that
b\cdot c\in X
too.

Now, for any
b\in\mathrm{Stab}(p)
we can find
c\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(c/A^{*},b)
does not fork over
A^{*}
— namely, choose
c^{*}\models\widehat{p}_{\mid A^{*},b^{*}}
. Therefore, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\subseteq X\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq X\cdot X^{-1}
.
∎

As an immediate corollary we get the following result:

Corollary 3.19
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
. Let
G
be a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
a locally atomic
N
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets invariant under left translations. Let
p
be an
N
-medium
N
-type with
p\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. Then,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
.

Proof.

If
p\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
, by
3.15
, we get that
\mathrm{St}(p)\subseteq p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}
. By
3.16
,
\mathrm{St}(p)
is a stable subset. Hence, by
3.18
, taking
\widehat{p}
a coheir of
\underline{p}
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}\subseteq(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
.
∎

Lemma 3.20
.

[
MOS20
, Lemma 2.11]

Let
G
be a piecewise
A^{*}
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
an
A^{*}
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
which is also invariant under left translations. Let
p
be an
A^{*}
-type and
W
a strictly
A^{*}
-medium
\bigwedge_{A^{*}}
-definable subset such that
p^{-1}W
is
A^{*}
-medium too. Suppose that there is an
A^{*}
-invariant global type
\widehat{p}
extending a partial type
\underline{p}
over
A^{*}
defining
p
. Then,
p\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(W)
.

Proof.

Take
(a^{*}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
such that
a^{*}_{i}\models\widehat{p}_{\mid A^{*},a^{*}_{0},\ldots,a^{*}_{i-1}}
. Then,
(a_{i})_{i\in\omega}
is an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence of realisations of
p
with
\mathrm{tp}(a_{1}/A^{*},a_{0})
non-forking. As
\mu
is invariant under left translations,
a^{-1}_{0}W
is wide. Since
p^{-1}W
is
A^{*}
-medium, we conclude that
a^{-1}_{0}W\cap a^{-1}_{1}W\not\in\mu
. Thus, by invariance under left translations,
a_{0}a^{-1}_{1}\in\mathrm{St}(W)
. By
3.14
(
2
) and
3.16
, we conclude that
p\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(A^{*})}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(W)
.
∎

For two subsets
X
and
Y
of a group
G
, recall that
[X:Y]\coloneqq\min\{|\Delta|\mathrel{:}X\subseteq\Delta Y\}
. Note that
[X^{-1}:Y^{-1}]=\min\{|\Delta|\mathrel{:}X\subseteq Y\Delta\}
.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A^{*}
-hyperdefinable group and
S\leq G
an
\bigwedge_{A^{*}}
-definable subgroup. Let
p\subseteq G
be an
A^{*}
-type such that there is an
A^{*}
-invariant global type
\widehat{p}
extending a partial type
\underline{p}
defining
p
over
A^{*}
.

(1)

If
[p:S]
is small, then
[p:S]=1
and
p^{-1}\cdot p\subseteq S
.

(2)

If
[p^{-1}:S]
is small, then
[p^{-1}:S]=1
and
p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq S
.

Proof.

Both are very similar, so we just prove (
1
). Let
(a_{i})_{i\in\alpha}
be a sequence in
p
such that
\alpha=[p:S]
and
a_{j}S\cap a_{i}S=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
, and
p\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in\alpha}a_{i}S
. Suppose that there is no
i\in\alpha
such that
\underline{(a_{i}S)\cap p}
is contained in
\widehat{p}
. Then, there are formulas
\varphi_{i}\in\underline{(a_{i}S)\cap p}
for each
i\in\alpha
such that
\{\neg\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in\alpha}\subseteq\widehat{p}
. In particular,
\{\neg\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in\alpha}\cup\underline{p}
is finitely satisfiable. Thus, by saturation, there is
c\in p
such that
c\notin\bigcup_{i\in\alpha}a_{i}S
, getting a contradiction. That concludes that there is
i\in\alpha
such that
\underline{(a_{i}S)\cap p}\subseteq\widehat{p}
. Since
a_{i}S\cap a_{j}S=\emptyset
for any
i\neq j
, we conclude that there is just one such
i\in\alpha
. Since
\widehat{p}
and
S
are
A^{*}
-invariant, we get that
a_{i}S
is
A^{*}
-invariant too. Indeed, take
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak{M}/A^{*})
arbitrary. Then,
\sigma[\underline{(a_{i}S)\cap p}]\subseteq\widehat{p}
defines
(\sigma(a_{i})S)\cap p
, concluding that
\sigma(a_{i})S=a_{i}S
. As
\sigma
is arbitrary, we conclude that
a_{i}S
is
A^{*}
-invariant. Thus,
a_{i}S
is
\bigwedge_{A^{*}}
-definable by
1.7
. As
(a_{i}S)\cap p\neq\emptyset
, by minimality of
p
,
p\subseteq a_{i}S
. So,
[p:S]=1
and
p^{-1}\cdot p\subseteq S
.

For (
2
), one argues analogously to point (
1
) but working with right cosets — as
[p^{-1}:S]=\min\{|\Delta|\mathrel{:}p\subseteq S\Delta\}
.
∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group,
S\leq G
an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subgroup and
V\subseteq G
an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable set. Suppose
[V:S]
is not small. Then, there is an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(a_{i})_{i\in\omega}
in
V
such that
a_{i}\cdot S\cap a_{j}\cdot S=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{G}
,
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{V}
be the interpretations in the monster model
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{C}}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{C}}
is the monster model,
[\mathbf{V}:\mathbf{S}]\notin\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}
. Recall that there is
\tau\in\mathbf{\mathbbm{O}\mathrm{n}}
depending only on
|A^{*}|
such that, for any sequence
(a^{*}_{i})_{i\in\tau}
of elements in
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{C}}
, there is an
A^{*}
-indiscernible sequence
(b^{*}_{j})_{j\in\omega}
of elements in
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{C}}
with the property that, for any
j_{1}<\cdots<j_{n}
,
\mathrm{tp}(a^{*}_{i_{1}},\ldots,a^{*}_{i_{n}}/A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(b^{*}_{j_{1}},\ldots,b^{*}_{j_{n}}/A^{*})
for some
i_{1}<\cdots<i_{n}
— see
[
TZ12
, Lemma 7.2.12]
. In particular, take
(a_{i})_{i\in\tau}
a sequence of hyperimaginaries of
\mathbf{V}
such that
a_{i}\cdot\mathbf{S}\cap a_{j}\cdot\mathbf{S}=\emptyset
for each
i\neq j
. Let
(a^{*}_{i})_{i\in\tau}
be representatives of
(a_{i})_{i\in\tau}
. Then, there is a sequence
(\widetilde{b}^{*}_{j})_{j\in\omega}
of
A^{*}
-indiscernible elements such that
\mathrm{tp}(\widetilde{b}^{*}_{0},\widetilde{b}^{*}_{1}/A^{*})=\mathrm{tp}(a^{*}_{j^{\prime}},a^{*}_{j^{\prime\prime}}/A^{*})
for some
j^{\prime}<j^{\prime\prime}
. Now, by
\kappa
-saturation of
\mathfrak{M}
, we can find
(b^{*}_{j})_{j\in\omega}
elements in
\mathfrak{M}
realising the same type that
(\widetilde{b}^{*}_{j})_{j\in\omega}
over
A^{*}
. So, the projections
b_{j}\in V
form an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{j})_{j\in\omega}
in
V
such that
b_{i}\cdot S\cap b_{j}\cdot S=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
.
∎

We now prove the Stabilizer Theorem for piecewise hyperdefinable groups. Below,
3.24
corresponds to
[
MOS20
, Theorem 2.12 (B2)]
;
3.25
corresponds to
[
Hru12
, Theorem 3.5]
and
[
MOS20
, Theorem 2.12 (B1)]
; and
3.25
(
c
) corresponds to
[
MOS20
, Proposition 2.14]
.

Theorem 3.23
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
. Let
G
be a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
a locally atomic
N
-invariant ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets invariant under left translations. Let
p\subseteq G
be an
N
-medium
N
-type. Assume that there is a wide
N
-type
q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
such that
p^{-1}\cdot q
is
N
-medium. Then,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=\mathrm{St}(q)^{4}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup of
G
without proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index. Furthermore:

(a)

\mathrm{Stab}(q)\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
.

(b)

Every strictly
N
-medium
N
-type of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
lies in
\mathrm{St}(p)
.

Proof.

We take
\widehat{p}
and
\widehat{q}
coheirs of
\underline{p}
and
\underline{q}
. By
3.11
,
q
is
N
-medium. By
3.20
,
p\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(q)
. Then,
\mathrm{St}(p)\subseteq p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(q)^{2}
. Indeed, for any
a,b\in p
we can find
c\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(c/N,a,b)
does not fork over
N
— simply, take
c^{*}
realising
\widehat{p}_{\mid N,a^{*},b^{*}}
. Thus,
ac^{-1},bc^{-1}\in\mathrm{St}(q)
, concluding that
ab^{-1}\in\mathrm{St}(q)^{2}
by
3.15
(
2
).

Since
q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
,
\mathrm{Stab}(q)\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.18
and
3.16
. In particular,
\mathrm{Stab}(q)\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}
by
3.15
(
2
). Thus,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}=\mathrm{St}(q)^{4}
is an
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup. Since
q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
, we have that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is wide.

Take an
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup
T\leq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
such that
[\mathrm{Stab}(p):T]
is small. Since
p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
,
[p^{-1}:T]
is also small. By
3.21
(
2
),
p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq T
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}\subseteq T
