. In other words,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
does not have proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index.

Finally, we prove property (
b
). Take
r\subset\mathrm{Stab}(p)
a strictly
N
-medium
N
-type. Set
c\in q
. Since
\mu
is locally atomic, there is
b\in r
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,c)\notin\mu
. By
3.10
,
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,c)
does not fork over
N
. Then, by
3.5
and
1.9
,
\mathrm{tp}(b^{-1}c^{-1}/N,c)
does not fork over
N
. Since
c,b\in\mathrm{Stab}(q)
, we have
b^{-1}c^{-1}\in\mathrm{Stab}(q)
. Write
r^{\prime}=\mathrm{tp}(b^{-1}c^{-1}/N)\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(q)
. By (a),
r^{\prime}\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. By
3.14
and
3.16
, we conclude that
b^{-1}=b^{-1}\cdot c^{-1}\cdot c\in\mathrm{St}(p)
, so
b\in\mathrm{St}(p)
. Since
r
is a type over
N
, we conclude
r\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
.
∎

Corollary 3.24
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
and
G
a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group. Let
\mu
be an
N
-invariant locally atomic ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
invariant under left and right translations. Let
p
be a wide type over
N
and assume that
p^{-1}\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
is
N
-medium. Then,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup of
G
without proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index such that every strictly
N
-medium
N
-type of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
lies in
\mathrm{St}(p)
.

Proof.

Take
a\in p
arbitrary. By the local atomic property, there is
b\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
is wide. By
3.11
,
p\cdot p^{-1}
is
N
-medium. Again by
3.11
but now using invariance under right translations, we get that
p
is
N
-medium. By
3.10
, it follows that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
does not fork over
N
. Consider the
N
-type
q=\mathrm{tp}(ba^{-1}/N)\subseteq p\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(N)}{\cdot}p^{-1}
.

By invariance under right translations, we know that
q
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable set. As
q\subseteq p\cdot p^{-1}
with
p\cdot p^{-1}

N
-medium, we have that
q
is
N
-medium too. Also, note that
p^{-1}\cdot q\subseteq p^{-1}\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
, so
p^{-1}\cdot q
is
N
-medium too. On the other hand, by
3.11
using invariance under right translations,
p^{-1}\cdot p
is
N
-medium, so
p\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.20
. By
3.16
and
3.14
(
3
),
p\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(N)}{\cdot}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
too, so
q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. By
3.23
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup of
G
without proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index such that every strictly
N
-medium
N
-type of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
lies in
\mathrm{St}(p)
.
∎

Theorem 3.25

(Stabilizer Theorem)
.

Let
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
and
G=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,X^{n}
a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group generated by a symmetric
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subset
X
. Let
\mu
be an
N
-invariant locally atomic ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
invariant under left translations. Let
p\subseteq X
be a wide type over
N
and assume that
X^{3}
is
N
-medium. Then,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is a wide and
N
-medium
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable normal subgroup of small index of
G
without proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index. Furthermore:

(a)

p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}=p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is a left coset of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
.

(b)

Every wide
N
-type of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is contained in
\mathrm{St}(p)
.

(c)

Assume
\mu
is also invariant under right translations. Then,
p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p=\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot p
is a right coset of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
.

Proof.

Take
a\in p
arbitrary. By the local atomic property, we find
b\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
is wide. By
3.11
,
X
is
N
-medium. As
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)\subseteq p\subseteq X
, we conclude that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
is
N
-medium, so
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
does not fork over
N
by
3.10
. Also,
\mathrm{tp}(a^{-1}b/N,a)=a^{-1}\cdot\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
is wide by left invariance of
\mu
, so
q=\mathrm{tp}(a^{-1}b/N)\subseteq p^{-1}\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}p
is wide. By
3.11
,
X^{2}
is
N
-medium, so
p^{2}
is
N
-medium too. Using any coheir extending
\underline{p^{-1}}
, we get
p^{-1}\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}p\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.20
. In particular,
q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. As
X
is symmetric,
p^{-1}\cdot q\subseteq p^{-1}\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p\subseteq X^{3}
, so
p^{-1}\cdot q
is
N
-medium. Thus, by
3.23
,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q)=\mathrm{St}(p)^{2}=\mathrm{St}(q)^{4}=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroup without proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index. By
3.23
(
b
), every strictly
N
-medium type of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is contained in
\mathrm{St}(p)
. Also,
\mathrm{Stab}(q)\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.23
(
a
).

We show that it is a normal subgroup of small index. First of all, note that
[X^{2}:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]
is small. Otherwise, by
3.22
, there is an
N
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{j})_{j\in\omega}
in
X^{2}
such that
b_{i}\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cap b_{j}\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
. Take
d\in p^{-1}
arbitrary. As
p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
, we get
(b_{i}\cdot p\cdot d)\cap(b_{j}\cdot p\cdot d)=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
, so
b_{i}\cdot p\cap b_{j}\cdot p=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
. As
X^{3}
is
N
-medium, that implies
b_{0}\cdot p\in\mu
, so
p\in\mu
by invariance under left translations, contradicting our hypotheses. For any
c\in X
, we have
[\mathrm{tp}(c/N):\mathrm{Stab}(p)]=1
by
3.21
(
1
), so
\mathrm{tp}(c/N)\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot\mathrm{tp}(c/N)^{-1}=c\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot c^{-1}
is
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable. As
X
is symmetric, we also get that
[p^{-1}:c\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot c^{-1}]=[p^{-1}\cdot c:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]\leq[X^{2}:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]
is small, so
p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq c\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot c^{-1}
by
3.21
(
2
). Therefore,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\subseteq c\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot c^{-1}
. As
c\in X
is arbitrary and
X
is symmetric,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=c\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot c^{-1}
. Thus,
X\subseteq\mathrm{N}_{G}(\mathrm{Stab}(p))
, concluding
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\trianglelefteq G
. Since we have proved that
[X:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]
is small, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
has small index by normality.

We show now property (
a
), i.e.
p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}=p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)=y\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
for any
y\in p
. As
(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}=\mathrm{Stab}(p)
, we have
p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
. As
[p:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]\leq[X:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]
is small, we get
[p:\mathrm{Stab}(p)]=1
by
3.21
(
1
). Thus,
p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)=y\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
. On the other hand, take
x\in y\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
arbitrary, so
x^{-1}=c\cdot y^{-1}
with
c\in\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q)
. By definition of
q
, we know that
q=\mathrm{tp}(a^{-1}b/N)
with
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
wide and
a,b\in p
. Take
z_{0}
such that
\mathrm{tp}(z_{0},y/N)=\mathrm{tp}(b,a/N)
. By
N
-invariance of
\mu
,
\mathrm{tp}(z_{0}/N,y)
is wide. By the local atomic property, we can find
z\in\mathrm{tp}(z_{0}/N,y)
such that
\mathrm{tp}(z/N,y,c)
is wide. Thus,
\mathrm{tp}(y^{-1}z/N,c)
is wide by invariance under left translations. By
3.10
,
\mathrm{tp}(y^{-1}z/N,c)
does not fork over
N
. Thus,
x^{-1}\cdot z=c\cdot y^{-1}z\in\mathrm{Stab}(q)\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. In particular, we conclude
x^{-1}\in\mathrm{St}(p)\cdot p^{-1}\subseteq p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p^{-1}
, so
x\in p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
.

As
X
is symmetric,
p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)\subseteq X^{3}
, so
p\cdot\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is
N
-medium. By
3.11
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is
N
-medium. Thus, we get property (
b
).

Finally, it remains to prove property (
c
). In other words, assuming that
\mu
is also invariant under right translations, we want to show that
p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p=\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot p=\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot y
for any
y\in p
. Take
a\in p
arbitrary and, using the local atomic property, find
b\in p
such that
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
is wide. As
p
is
N
-medium, by
3.10
,
\mathrm{tp}(b/N,a)
does not fork over
N
. Consider
q_{2}=\mathrm{tp}(ba^{-1}/N)\subseteq p\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(N)}{\cdot}p^{-1}
. Then, by invariance under right translations,
q_{2}
is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable set. As
q_{2}\subseteq X^{2}
and
p^{-1}\cdot q_{2}\subseteq X^{3}
, we conclude that
q_{2}
and
p^{-1}\cdot q_{2}
are
N
-medium. As
p\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
by
3.18
, we get using
3.16
and
3.14
(
3
) that
p\prescript{}{\mathrm{nf}(N)}{\cdot}p^{-1}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
too, so
q_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. By
3.23
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q_{2})
and
\mathrm{Stab}(q_{2})\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
. Take
x\in\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot y
arbitrary, so
x=cy
with
c\in\mathrm{Stab}(p)=\mathrm{Stab}(q_{2})
and
y\in p
. Find
z_{0}
such that
\mathrm{tp}(z_{0},y/N)=\mathrm{tp}(a,b/N)
, so
\mathrm{tp}(y/N,z_{0})
is wide. Using the local atomic property, we may find
y_{1}\in\mathrm{tp}(y/N,z_{0})
such that
\mathrm{tp}(y_{1}/N,z_{0},c)
is wide. Take
z
such that
\mathrm{tp}(z,y/N,c)=\mathrm{tp}(z_{0},y_{1}/N,c)
. Thus,
yz^{-1}\in q_{2}
and
\mathrm{tp}(y/N,z,c)
is wide. In particular, as
X^{2}
is
N
-medium, by
3.10
,
\mathrm{tp}(yz^{-1}/N,c)
does not fork over
N
. Thus,
x\cdot z^{-1}=c\cdot yz^{-1}\in\mathrm{Stab}(q_{2})\cdot_{\mathrm{nf}(N)}q_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{St}(p)
, concluding
x\in p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p
. As
x
is arbitrary,
\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot y\subseteq p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p
. As
p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot y
, we get
p\cdot p^{-1}\cdot p=\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot p=\mathrm{Stab}(p)\cdot y
for any
y\in p
.
∎

Remark 3.26
.

()

The main improvement of
3.25
with respect to the original formulations studied in
[
Hru12
,
MOS20
]
is that we do not require invariance of
\mu
under right translations. In both papers, while they preferably considered ideals invariant under two-sided translations, the authors already anticipated that it should be possible to weaken the invariance under right translations hypothesis, perhaps losing a few properties of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)
. We have shown that, in fact, it can be completely eliminated without significant consequences. Indeed, we only use invariance under right translations for (
c
), but this is essentially replaced by (
a
).

(2)

Although in
[
MOS20
, Theorem 2.12]
it was assumed that
X^{4}
is
N
-medium, we only need
N
-mediumness of
X^{3}
.

(3)

Note that the locally atomic property is mostly used only for subsets of
p
. The only time when we apply the locally atomic property for other subsets is in the proof of
3.23
(
b
). Thus, when
\mu
only satisfies the locally atomic property for subsets of
p
, we only miss (
b
).

For property (
b
), we have used the locally atomic property for subsets of
\mathrm{Stab}(p)=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
. However, in fact, it suffices to assume it only for subsets of
p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
. Indeed, by
3.25
(
a
) and invariance under left translations, if
V\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}(p)
is
\bigwedge_{B}
-definable and wide, then
y\cdot V\subseteq p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
is
\bigwedge_{B,y}
-definable and wide for any
y\in p
. By the locally atomic property on
p\cdot p\cdot p^{-1}
, there is
b\in V
such that
\mathrm{tp}(yb/B,y)
is wide, concluding that
\mathrm{tp}(b/B)
is wide by invariance under left translations.

(4)

It suffices to have
\mu
defined only on
X^{3}
, as, in that case, we may extend the ideal by taking the one generated by the finite unions of left translates of elements of
\mu
. As
\mu
is invariant under left-translations within
X^{3}
(i.e.
V\in\mu
if and only if
gV\in\mu
for any
V\subseteq X^{3}
and
g\in G
such that
gV\subseteq X^{3}
), this extension coincides with
\mu
inside of
X^{3}
, so we can apply the Stabilizer
3.25
by
3.26
(
3
).

Proposition 3.27
.

[
Hru12
, Corollary 3.11]
&
[
MOS20
, Proposition 2.13]
Let
\mathfrak{N}\prec\mathfrak{M}
with
|N|<\lambda
and
G=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,X^{n}
a piecewise
N
-hyperdefinable group generated by a symmetric
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subset
X
. Let
\mu
be an
N
-invariant locally atomic ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets of
G
invariant under left translations such that
X
is wide and
X^{3}
is
N
-medium. Then,
\mu
is
N
-medium on
G
.

Proof.

By the locally atomic property, there is a wide
N
-type
p\subseteq X
. By the Stabilizer
3.25
,
S\coloneqq\mathrm{Stab}(p)=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
is an
N
-medium normal subgroup of small index. Let
Y(\overline{b})\subseteq G
be
\bigwedge_{N,\bar{b}}
-definable with
|\overline{b}|<\lambda
small. Let
(\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be an
N
-indiscernible sequence with
\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}/N)=\mathrm{tp}(\overline{b}_{i}/N)
. Suppose that
Y(\overline{b})\notin\mu
. By the locally atomic property of
\mu
, there is a wide
N,\overline{b}
-type
q(\overline{b})\subseteq Y(\overline{b})
. By
3.21
(
1
), there is
a
such that
q(\overline{b})\subseteq a\cdot S
. Let
(\overline{b}^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be an
N,a^{*}
-indiscernible sequence realising
\mathrm{tp}((\overline{b}_{i})_{i\in\omega}/N)
. By invariance under left translations,
a^{-1}q(\overline{b})\notin\mu
. As
S
is
N
-medium,
a^{-1}q(\overline{b}^{\prime}_{0})\cap a^{-1}q(\overline{b}^{\prime}_{1})\notin\mu
. Thus, by
N
-invariance and invariance under left translations, we get that
q(\overline{b}_{0})\cap q(\overline{b}_{1})\notin\mu
, concluding
Y(\overline{b}_{0})\cap Y(\overline{b}_{1})\notin\mu
.
∎

3.5.
Near-subgroups

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group and
\mu
an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets invariant under left translations with
\kappa\geq\lambda>|\mathtt{L}|+|A|
. A
\mu
-near-subgroup
of
G
over
A
is a wide
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable symmetric set
X
generating
G
such that
\mu_{\mid X^{3}}
is locally atomic and
A
-medium. We say that
X
is a near-subgroup if it is a
\mu
-near-subgroup for some ideal.

Theorem 3.28
.

Let
G
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group,
\mu
an ideal of
\bigwedge_{<\lambda}
-definable subsets and
X
a
\mu
-near-subgroup over
A
. Then, there is a wide
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable normal subgroup of small index
S
contained in
X^{4}
and contained in every
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subgroup of small index. Furthermore,
S=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}
and
ppp^{-1}=pS=yS
for any
y\in p
, where
p\subseteq X
is a wide type over an elementary substructure of size
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
.

Proof.

By Löwenheim-Skolem Theorem
[
TZ12
, Theorem 2.3.1]
, we find an elementary substructure
\mathfrak{N}\preceq\mathfrak{M}
containing
A^{*}
with
|N|=|A^{*}|+|\mathtt{L}|<\lambda
. As
A^{*}\subseteq N
,
\mu_{\mid X^{3}}
is
N
-medium. By the locally atomic property, there is a wide
N
-type
p\subseteq X
. Applying the Stabilizer
3.25
(and
3.26
(
3
)), we conclude that there is a wide
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable normal subgroup
S\trianglelefteq G
of small index which does not have proper
\bigwedge_{N}
-definable subgroups of small index. Furthermore,
S=\mathrm{Stab}(p)=(p\cdot p^{-1})^{2}\subseteq X^{4}
and
ppp^{-1}=pS=yS
for any
y\in p
. As
A^{*}\subseteq N
, we conclude.
∎

Theorem 3.29
.

Let
G=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,X^{n}
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group generated by a near-subgroup
X
over
A
. Assume that
X^{n}
is an approximate subgroup for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,
G
has a connected Lie model
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
K\subseteq X^{2n+4}
such that

(a)

H\cap X^{2n}
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and commensurable to
X^{n}
,

(b)

\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity in
L
,

(c)

H
is generated by
H\cap X^{2n+4}
, and

(d)

\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and proper from the logic topology using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.

Proof.

As
X
is a near-subgroup, by
3.28
,
G^{00}_{N}\subseteq X^{4}
with
|N|\leq|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
. As
X^{n}
is an approximate subgroup,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is an approximate subgroup. Thus,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a neighbourhood of the identity by the Generic Set Lemma (
2.22
). Then, by
2.34
, we can find a connected Lie core
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
K\subseteq X^{2n}G^{00}_{N}\subseteq X^{2n+4}
.

(a)

Since
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
is compact and
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}
is an open subgroup in the global logic topology, we conclude that
\left[\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}:\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{A}$}}\right]
is finite. As
G^{00}_{A}\leq H
, we get that
[X^{n}:H]
is finite. Thus, by
[
Mac23
, Lemma 2.2, Lemma 2.3]
,
H\cap X^{2n}
is an approximate subgroup commensurable to
X^{n}
.

(b)

As
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity and
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is an open subgroup in the global logic topology, we conclude that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity. Recall that
\widetilde{\pi}_{H/K}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}\rightarrow L
given by
\pi_{H/K}=\widetilde{\pi}_{H/K}\circ\pi_{G/G^{00}_{N}}
is a continuous, closed, open and proper onto group homomorphism. Thus, we conclude that
\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity.

(c)

Since
L
is connected and
\pi_{H/K}[X^{2n}\cap H]
is a neighbourhood of the identity,
\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
generates
L
. Therefore, we have that
\pi^{-1}_{H/K}[\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]]=(H\cap X^{2n})\cdot K
generates
H
. Now,
K\subseteq H\cap X^{2n+4}
, so
(H\cap X^{2n})\cdot K\subseteq(H\cap X^{2n+4})^{2}
, concluding that
H\cap X^{2n+4}
generates
H
.

(d)

By
1.53
, the global logic topology of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is given using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters, so
H\cap X^{2n}
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and
\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and closed using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.∎

Alternatively, using Gleason-Yamabe-Kreitlon
2.4
rather than Gleason-Yamabe
2.3
, we get the following variation which provides some extra control over some of the parameters.

Theorem 3.30
.

There are functions
c:\ \mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
d:\ \mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that the following holds for any
\kappa
-saturated
\mathtt{L}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
and any set of parameters
A
with
\kappa>|\mathtt{L}|+|A|
:

Let
G=\underrightarrow{\lim}\,X^{n}
be a piecewise
A
-hyperdefinable group generated by a near-subgroup
X
over
A
. Assume that
X^{n}
is a
k
-approximate subgroup for some
n
and
k
. Then,
G
has a Lie model
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
K\subseteq X^{12n+4}
and
\dim(L)\leq d(k)
such that

(a)

H\cap X^{2n}
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and
c(k)
-commensurable to
X^{n}
,

(b)

\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity in
L
,

(c)

H
is generated by
H\cap X^{12n+4}
, and

(d)

\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and proper from the logic topology using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.

Proof.

As
X
is a near-subgroup, by
3.28
,
G^{00}_{N}\subseteq X^{4}
with
|N|\leq|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
. As
X^{n}
is a
k
-approximate subgroup,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is an approximate subgroup. Thus,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a neighbourhood of the identity by the Generic Set Lemma (
2.22
), and, in particular,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a locally compact topological group with the global logic topology. Thus,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is contained in the interior,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
, of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{3n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{3n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{3n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{3n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
in the global logic topology. Now, we obviously have that
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle U^{2}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle U^{2}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U^{2}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U^{2}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{6n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{6n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{6n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{6n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
, which is covered by
k^{5}
left translates of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X^{n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
. Hence,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is an open precompact
k^{5}
-approximate subgroup.

Let
c_{0}
and
d_{0}
be the functions provided by Gleason-Yamabe-Kreitlon
2.4
. Applying
2.4
, we get a Lie model
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
G^{00}_{N}\leq K\subseteq U^{4}\subseteq X^{12n}G^{00}_{N}\subseteq X^{12n+4}
and
\dim(L)\leq d(k)\coloneqq d_{0}(k^{5})
such that
H\cap U^{4}
generates
H
and is
c_{0}(k^{5})
-commensurable to
U
. Thus,
H\cap X^{12n+4}
generates
H
and
c_{0}(k^{5})
left translates of
H\cap X^{12n+4}
cover
X^{n}
. As
X^{n}
is a
k
-approximate subgroup,
k^{15}
left translates of
X^{n}
cover
X^{16n}\supseteq H\cap X^{12n+4}
. By
[
Mac23
, Lemma 2.2]
, we get that
k^{15}
left translates of
H\cap X^{2n}
cover
H\cap X^{12n+4}
, so
H\cap X^{2n}
and
X^{n}
are
c(k)
-commensurable, where
c(k)\coloneqq c_{0}(k^{5})k^{15}
.

Now,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H\cap X^{2n}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity in the global logic topology. Recall that
\widetilde{\pi}_{H/K}:\ \mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}\rightarrow L
given by
\pi_{H/K}=\widetilde{\pi}_{H/K}\circ\pi_{G/G^{00}_{N}}
is a continuous, closed, open and proper onto group homomorphism. Thus,
\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity of
L
.

By
1.53
, the global logic topology of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle G^{00}_{N}$}}
is given using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters, so
H\cap X^{2n}
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and
\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and proper using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.
∎

We conclude applying
3.29
to the case of rough approximate subgroups. Recall that a
T
-rough
k
-approximate subgroup
of a group
G
is a subset
X\subseteq G
such that
X^{2}\subseteq\Delta XT
with
|\Delta|\leq k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
and
1\in T\subseteq G
.

Let
G
be a group and
X\subseteq G
a symmetric subset. For some fixed
n
, assume
X^{n}
is a
T_{i}
-rough
k
-approximate subgroup for a sequence
(T_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of thickenings satisfying that

(i)

it decreases in doubling scales, i.e.
T_{i+1}T^{-1}_{i+1}\subseteq T_{i}
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and

(ii)

it is asymptotically normalised by
X
, i.e.
x^{-1}T_{i+1}x\subseteq T_{i}
for each
x\in X
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assuming saturation and
\bigwedge
-definability, we have that
T=\bigcap T_{i}
is an
\bigwedge
-definable subgroup of
G
normalised by
X
and
X^{n}
is a
T
-rough
k
-approximate subgroup.

Theorem 3.31

(Rough Lie Model Theorem)
.

Let
G
be an
A
-definable group,
T\leq G
an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subgroup and
X\subseteq G
a symmetric
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subset. Write
\widetilde{G}
for the subgroup generated by
X
. Assume that
X
normalises
T
,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.81949ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.73616ptT$}}
is a near-subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.81949ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.73616ptT$}}
and
X^{n}
is a
T
-rough approximate subgroup for some
n
. Then,
\widetilde{G}T\leq G
has a connected Lie model
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
T\subseteq K\subseteq X^{2n+4}T
such that

(a)

H\cap X^{2n}T
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and commensurable to
X^{n}T
,

(b)

\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity in
L
,

(c)

H
is generated by
H\cap X^{2n+4}T
, and

(d)

\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and proper from the logic topology using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.

Alternatively, using
3.30
:

Theorem 3.32

(Rough Lie model Theorem, version 2)
.

There are functions
c:\ \mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
d:\ \mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that the following holds for any
\kappa
-saturated
\mathtt{L}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
and any set of parameters
A
with
\kappa>|\mathtt{L}|+|A|
:

Let
G
be an
A
-definable group,
T\leq G
an
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subgroup and
X\subseteq G
a symmetric
\bigwedge_{A}
-definable subset. Write
\widetilde{G}
for the subgroup generated by
X
. Assume that
X
normalises
T
,
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.81949ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.73616ptT$}}
is a near-subgroup of
\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle\kern 1.00006ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.81949ptT$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{G}$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.73616ptT$}}
and
X^{n}
is a
T
-rough
k
-approximate subgroup for some
n
and
k
. Then,
\widetilde{G}T\leq G
has a Lie model
\pi_{H/K}:\ H\rightarrow L=\mathchoice{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\displaystyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\displaystyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\textstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle K$}}{\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle H$}\kern-1.00006pt/\kern-1.00006pt\lower 1.07639pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K$}}
with
T\subseteq K\subseteq X^{12n+4}T
and
\dim(L)\leq d(k)
such that

(a)

H\cap X^{2n}T
is
\bigwedge_{|A|+|\mathtt{L}|}
-definable and
c(k)
-commensurable to
X^{n}T
,

(b)

\pi_{H/K}[H\cap X^{2n}]
is a compact neighbourhood of the identity in
L
,

(c)

H
is generated by
H\cap X^{12n+4}T
, and

(d)

\pi_{H/K}
is continuous and proper from a logic topology using
|A|+|\mathtt{L}|
many parameters.
